<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a negative answer to Zariski's multiplicity question in four complex variables. We construct two reduced convergent holomorphic function germs with isolated critical points and multiplicities four and five whose zero-set germs are ambiently homeomorphic.

- **stmt-thm-main** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are reduced convergent holomorphic function germs $f_4,f_5\colon(\mathbb C^4,0)\to(\mathbb C,0)$, each with an isolated critical point at the origin, such that $$\operatorname{ord}_0f_4=4,\qquad \operatorname{ord}_0f_5=5,$$ and a homeomorphism germ of ambient pairs $$(\mathbb C^4,V(f_4),0) \;\cong\; (\mathbb C^4,V(f_5),0).$$
  - Explanation: Ambient topology of the zero set does not determine multiplicity for the constructed isolated holomorphic germs.
- **stmt-lem-plane-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-plane-data)): Each $\pi_i$ is a finite map germ of degree four. The curves $d_i=0$ are reduced, have isolated singularities at the origin, and have the same six-branch embedded type. Their integral Milnor Seifert forms are isometric, and each has Milnor number $51$. Let $\Phi_5(t)=t^4+t^3+t^2+t+1$. If $M_h$ is the monodromy on $H_{\mathbb Q}=H\otimes\mathbb Q$, then $$\dim_{\mathbb Q}\ker(M_h-I)=2,\qquad \dim_{\mathbb Q}\ker\Phi_5(M_h)=4.$$ The monodromy on either $D_i\otimes\mathbb C$ is semisimple at eigenvalue $1$ and at every primitive fifth root of unity.
  - Explanation: The pulled-back plane curves share embedded type and integral Seifert forms, while their projections remain distinct data.
- **stmt-lem-cover-adjunction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-adjunction)): For $i=0,1$, the page maps induce $\pi_{i*}:D_i\to H$ and a full-preimage transfer $\pi_i^!:H\to D_i$ satisfying $$\pi_{i*}\pi_i^!=4\,\mathrm{id}_H .$$ If $s_h,s_{d_i}$ are the Seifert forms computed by ordered filling-chain intersection in the total Milnor tubes, then $$s_{d_i}(\pi_i^!a,b)=s_h(a,\pi_{i*}b),\qquad s_{d_i}(b,\pi_i^!a)=s_h(\pi_{i*}b,a).$$ Consequently, after tensoring with $\mathbb Q$, $$D_{i,\mathbb Q}=(\ker\pi_{i*})_{\mathbb Q} \ \perp\ \pi_i^!H_{\mathbb Q},\qquad \operatorname{pr}_i(l)=l-\tfrac14\pi_i^!\pi_{i*}l$$ is the projection onto $(\ker\pi_{i*})_{\mathbb Q}$ for both orders of the form.
  - Explanation: Pushforward and full-preimage transfer give the rational orthogonal projection needed in the gluing calculation.
- **stmt-lem-simultaneous-parameters** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-parameters)): For every sufficiently large integer $r$, choose an integer $n_0$ such that $$n_0\equiv1\pmod{2^r},\qquad n_0\equiv2\pmod5,\qquad n_0>5\cdot2^r,$$ and define $$\begin{aligned} n_1&=n_0+5\cdot2^r,& a&=(n_0^2-1)/4,& b&=(n_1^2-1)/4,\\ \alpha&=\frac{4b}{a+b},& \beta&=\frac{4a}{a+b}. \end{aligned}$$ Then $a,b$ are positive comparable integers, $n_0,n_1\equiv1\pmod4$, and $\gcd(n_0,n_1)=1$. Moreover $$\alpha+\beta=4,\qquad a\alpha=b\beta,\qquad \frac{(a-2)\alpha}{4}>4,\qquad \frac{(b-2)\beta}{4}>4.$$
  - Explanation: A common congruence-based parameter choice supplies both analytic growth bounds and coprime arithmetic indices.
- **stmt-prop-ambient-orders** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-ambient-orders)): For the parameters of Lemma <a href="#lem:simultaneous-parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:simultaneous-parameters">4</a> and every sufficiently small $s>0$, each $C_{i,s}$ is a smooth complex fourfold near the origin, and $f_{i,s}$ has an isolated critical point there, as proved in Lemma <a href="#lem:isolation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:isolation">9</a>. After solving the smooth ambient equations in analytic local coordinates, $f_{0,s}$ has order five and $f_{1,s}$ has order four. Both are reduced convergent germs.
  - Explanation: Analytic elimination in smooth ambient charts exhibits distinct ordinary orders; isolation also gives reducedness.
- **stmt-thm-tube** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-tube)): For sufficiently large $a,b$ satisfying $$\frac{(a-2)\alpha}{k}>L_0,\qquad \frac{(b-2)\beta}{k}>L_0,$$ where $L_0=4$ is the gradient exponent in <a href="#eq:plane-gradient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:plane-gradient">[eq:plane-gradient]</a>, and for sufficiently small $s>0$, the germ $f_s:C_s\to\mathbb C$ has its sole critical point at the origin in a fixed small tube. Its integral Milnor lattice, in rational coordinates, is $$\Lambda_\pi= \left\{(\operatorname{pr}l,z): l\in D,\ z\in H\otimes E,\quad \pi_*l\bmod kH=(1\otimes\tau)z\right\} \subset K_\mathbb Q\oplus(H\otimes E)_\mathbb Q. \label{eq:tube-1}$$ The rational orthogonal filling form on these coordinates is $$-s_d\|_K\ \perp\ (s_h\otimes e). \label{eq:tube-2}$$ The lattice and form are those of a genuine local Milnor page of $f_s$ in the smooth ambient germ $C_s$.
  - Explanation: The actual local Milnor lattice is determined by a boundary residue condition, with separately signed inner and exterior forms.
- **stmt-lem-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary)): For every sufficiently small fixed $s>0$, the map $f_s$ on the compact outer lateral boundary of $X_s$ over $\{\|v\|\le\delta\}$ is proper, and its restriction to every stratum has real rank two throughout that disk. In particular, all active lateral faces and corners of <a href="#eq:tube-4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tube-4">[eq:tube-4]</a> are independent of the horizontal face over $\|f_s\|=\delta$. The horizontal boundary map is proper on the union of lateral strata and a submersion on each stratum.
  - Explanation: Proper stratified boundary regularity permits horizontal boundary fibrations on the chosen tube.
- **stmt-lem-continuations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-continuations)): For sufficiently small $s>0$, the exterior total/page pairs continue to $s=0$. On the inner part, the sublevel totals and horizontal-boundary fibrations continue from $h+P$ to $h$ through $h+\theta P$, $0\le\theta\le1$. These continuations preserve the complex value on the horizontal boundary and agree on $J$ up to isotopy through their two-parameter rectangle.* *They can be chosen to preserve the label $p_0$, the coordinate $w_0$, and the exact positive $y$-angle $\phi$ on the interface circles characterized by $$p=\Phi(p_0,q),\qquad w=w_0,\qquad y=M\lambda^\alpha e^{i\phi},\qquad h(p)+\theta P(y,z,w)=c.$$ Here $z=R_s(p,w_0)/y$, and $q$ is the unique value near $c$ that satisfies these equations. The inner continuation is an isotopy of sublevel totals; value preservation is required on its horizontal boundary, not through its critical region.
  - Explanation: The inner and exterior models continue compatibly while preserving labeled interface circles and horizontal complex values.
- **stmt-lem-isolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-isolation)): For sufficiently large $a,b$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:tube" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tube">6</a>, the only critical point of $f_s$ in <a href="#eq:tube-4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tube-4">[eq:tube-4]</a> for all sufficiently small $s>0$ is the origin.
  - Explanation: The gradient estimate and strict exponent bounds exclude nonorigin critical points in the tube.
- **stmt-lem-acyclic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-acyclic)): There are natural identifications $$H_3(W)=K,\qquad H_2(W)=H/\pi_*D,\qquad \Psi:H_3(W;\mathbb Z/k)\hookrightarrow D/kD. \label{eq:tube-11}$$ The large total $\widehat\mathcal T_s$ is acyclic.
  - Explanation: Circle collapse identifies inner homology and the mod-covering-degree injection; exterior attachment preserves acyclicity.
- **stmt-lem-local** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local)): The middle homology and filling pairing of the page of <a href="#eq:tube-4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tube-4">[eq:tube-4]</a> are naturally those of a sufficiently small ordinary Milnor representative of $f_s$.
  - Explanation: Relative homology and boundary regularity identify the large representative with ordinary local Milnor data.
- **stmt-lem-mv** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mv)): The full page has an exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow H_3(\mathcal T_s) \xrightarrow{\partial} H\otimes E \xrightarrow{\overline{1\otimes\tau}}H/\pi_*D. \label{eq:tube-13}$$ The image of the exterior page in $H_3(\mathcal T_s)$ maps isomorphically under $\partial$ to $H\otimes\ker\tau$.
  - Explanation: The signed exterior cut determines which tensor-product classes extend across the inner piece.
- **stmt-lem-bock** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bock)): For $b\in H/kH=H_2(J)$, and any $\eta\in H_3(J;\mathbb Z/k)$ with $\beta_{\rm Bock}\eta=b$, $$\Psi(i_*\eta)=\pi^!b\pmod{kD}, \label{eq:tube-14}$$ with the orientation of $\Psi$ fixed after <a href="#eq:tube-12" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tube-12">[eq:tube-12]</a>.
  - Explanation: The oriented coefficient Bockstein agrees with the full finite-map transfer.
- **stmt-lem-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice)): In these rational coordinates, the image of $H_3(\mathcal T_s)$ is exactly the lattice $$\Lambda_\pi= \left\{(\operatorname{pr}l,z): l\in D,\ z\in H\otimes E,\quad \pi_*l\bmod kH=(1\otimes\tau)z\right\} \subset K_\mathbb Q\oplus(H\otimes E)_\mathbb Q$$ from <a href="#eq:tube-1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tube-1">[eq:tube-1]</a>, where $\operatorname{pr}l=l-k^{-1}\pi^!\pi_*l$.
  - Explanation: Subtracting an interface cap determines the fractional inner coordinate and the exact integral residue lattice.
- **stmt-lem-exterior** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior)): The $K_\mathbb Q$ and $(H\otimes E)_\mathbb Q$ summands are orthogonal for the tube filling form. The second summand has form $s_h\otimes e$.
  - Explanation: Separated fillings yield orthogonality; lifting to the cyclic cover computes the exterior product form.
- **stmt-lem-inner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-inner)): The restriction of the filling form to $K$ is $-s_d\|_K$.
  - Explanation: The normal-disk intersection reverses the plane filling sign on the pushforward kernel.
- **stmt-lem-parameter-indices** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-indices)): For the parameters fixed in Lemma <a href="#lem:simultaneous-parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:simultaneous-parameters">4</a>, put $$g=\gcd(a,b),\qquad m=4ab+a+b.$$ Then $a,b\equiv2\pmod5$, $5\nmid g$, and $g\ge 2^{r-1}>5$. For each $q\in\{1,4\}$, there are positive integers $i,j$ satisfying $$i\equiv j\pmod4,\qquad \frac{bi+aj}{m}=\frac q5 .$$
  - Explanation: The fixed parameters admit positive congruent indices for the required primitive characters.
- **stmt-lem-arith-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-boundary)): The classes $b_0,b_1$ are fixed by the page monodromy, and $$\label{eq:arith-boundary-pairings} \begin{aligned} e(b_j,b_j)&=-\frac{n_j^2}{4},& e(b_0,b_1)&=-\frac14,\\ 4e(b_j,x)&\equiv-\tau(x)\pmod4&&(x\in E). \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The axis boundary classes are monodromy fixed and their rational pairings retain the lens-space residue.
- **stmt-lem-arith-local-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-local-splitting)): For every rational prime $\ell\nmid n_j$, the lattice $E_{\mathbb Z_\ell}$ has the following $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module splitting, orthogonal for the $\mathbb Q_\ell$-valued form $e$: $$E_{\mathbb Z_\ell}= \mathbb Z_\ell v_j\perp U_{j,\ell}, \qquad U_{j,\ell}\subset\ker(\tau_{\mathbb Z_\ell}).$$ For $\nu=0,1$ this induces an integral Seifert isometry $$\label{eq:arith-local-alpha} \begin{split} \alpha_{\nu,j}\colon &(D_\nu,-s_{d_\nu})_{\mathbb Z_\ell} \perp(H\otimes U_{j,\ell},s_h\otimes e) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\Lambda_\nu,s_\nu)_{\mathbb Z_\ell},\\ &(l,u)\longmapsto \bigl(\operatorname{pr}_\nu l,\ \pi_{\nu*}l\otimes v_j+u\bigr). \end{split}$$ It also holds over $\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: Normalized boundary classes give integral orthogonal splittings at primes where the corresponding index is a unit.
- **stmt-lem-arith-discrepancy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-discrepancy)): The rational Seifert isometry $\Delta=J_0^{-1}J_1$ acts as the identity outside $F_{0,1}\oplus F_{0,5}$. Its restriction to $F_{0,1}$ lifts to the rational points of the spin group of $B_0\|F_{0,1}$. Its restriction to $F_{0,5}$ lies in the special unitary group of the $L/L^+$ Hermitian form.
  - Explanation: The discrepancy is confined to primary sectors and has the spin and special-unitary lifts needed for correction.
- **stmt-lem-arith-real-signs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arith-real-signs)): The quadratic form $B_\nu\|F_{\nu,1}$ is indefinite and has dimension at least five. The Hermitian form on $F_{\nu,5}$ has dimension at least two over $L$ and is indefinite at the real place of $L^+$ indexed by the pair $\{\zeta,\bar\zeta\}$. Both assertions hold for $\nu=0,1$.
  - Explanation: Holomorphic and antiholomorphic classes give real indefiniteness of the correction forms.
- **stmt-prop-arith-integral-congruence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-arith-integral-congruence)): For the two germs constructed above, the two integral Milnor Seifert forms $(\Lambda_0,s_0)$ and $(\Lambda_1,s_1)$ are isometric.
  - Explanation: Strong approximation patches complementary local isometries while preserving the full integral lattice.

          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $a,b$ satisfying $$\\frac{(a-2)\\alpha}{k}>L_0,\\qquad\n \\frac{(b-2)\\beta}{k}>L_0,$$ where $L_0=4$ is the gradient exponent in <a href=\"#eq:plane-gradient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:plane-gradient\">[eq:plane-gradient]</a>, and for sufficiently small $s>0$, the germ $f_s:C_s\\to\\mathbb C$ has its sole critical point at the origin in a fixed small tube. Its integral Milnor lattice, in rational coordinates, is $$\\Lambda_\\pi=\n\\left\\{(\\operatorname{pr}l,z): l\\in D,\\ z\\in H\\otimes E,\\quad\n\\pi_*l\\bmod kH=(1\\otimes\\tau)z\\right\\}\n\\subset K_\\mathbb Q\\oplus(H\\otimes E)_\\mathbb Q.\n\\label{eq:tube-1}$$ The rational orthogonal filling form on these coordinates is $$-s_d|_K\\ \\perp\\ (s_h\\otimes e).\n\\label{eq:tube-2}$$ The lattice and form are those of a genuine local Milnor page of $f_s$ in the smooth ambient germ $C_s$.",
          "source_id": "PM-059.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently small fixed $s>0$, the map $f_s$ on the compact outer lateral boundary of $X_s$ over $\\{|v|\\le\\delta\\}$ is proper, and its restriction to every stratum has real rank two throughout that disk.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-continuations",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently small $s>0$, the exterior total/page pairs continue to $s=0$. On the inner part, the sublevel totals and horizontal-boundary fibrations continue from $h+P$ to $h$ through $h+\\theta P$, $0\\le\\theta\\le1$. These continuations preserve the complex value on the horizontal boundary and agree on $J$ up to isotopy through their two-parameter rectangle.*\n\n*They can be chosen to preserve the label $p_0$, the coordinate $w_0$, and the exact positive $y$-angle $\\phi$ on the interface circles characterized by $$p=\\Phi(p_0,q),\\qquad w=w_0,\\qquad\n y=M\\lambda^\\alpha e^{i\\phi},\\qquad h(p)+\\theta P(y,z,w)=c.$$ Here $z=R_s(p,w_0)/y$, and $q$ is the unique value near $c$ that satisfies these equations. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Reduced hypersurface germ** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The hypersurface germ $V(f)$ is *reduced* when $f$ has no repeated irreducible factor.
- **Multiplicity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its *multiplicity* is $\operatorname{ord}_0f$, the least total degree of a nonzero term of $f$.
- **Ambient homeomorphism** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Two hypersurface germs are *ambiently homeomorphic* if a homeomorphism between neighborhoods of the origin, fixing the origin, takes one zero set onto the other.
- **Ordered filling convention** ([source](#sec:plane-cover)): Throughout, $s(a,b)$ is the ordered intersection of a filling of $a$ on a reference page with a filling of the positive neighboring-page transport of $b$, in the acyclic total Milnor tube.
- **Prepared graph equation** ([source](#sec:tube)): Write $d=h\circ\pi$, and let $$R(p,w)=w^k+\sum_{j<k}A_j(p)w^j,\qquad k=4,$$ be its prepared equation, so $A_j(0)=0$ and $R(0,w)=w^k$.
- **Cone-page rational filling form** ([source](#sec:tube)): The cyclic cover of the nonvertex cone gives a full-lift map $\operatorname{tr}:E\to H_1(\widetilde Q;\mathbb Z)$; define $$e(v,v')=\frac1k\widetilde s(\operatorname{tr}v,\operatorname{tr}v').$$
- **Coefficient Bockstein convention** ([source](#sec:integral-lattice)): Our convention is $$\beta_{\rm Bock}[\overline C]=[\partial C/k]$$ when $C$ is an integral lift of a mod-$k$ cycle $\overline C$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arith-integral-congruence-2): Ambient topology of the zero set does not determine multiplicity for the constructed isolated holomorphic germs. -> Ambient topology of the zero set does not determine multiplicity for the constructed isolated holomorphic germs.
- **step-2** [claim](#stmt-lem-plane-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plane-data): The pulled-back plane curves share embedded type and integral Seifert forms, while their projections remain distinct data. -> The pulled-back plane curves share embedded type and integral Seifert forms, while their projections remain distinct data.
- **step-3** [claim](#stmt-lem-cover-adjunction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-adjunction): Pushforward and full-preimage transfer give the rational orthogonal projection needed in the gluing calculation. -> Pushforward and full-preimage transfer give the rational orthogonal projection needed in the gluing calculation.
- **step-4** [claim](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simultaneous-parameters): A common congruence-based parameter choice supplies both analytic growth bounds and coprime arithmetic indices. -> A common congruence-based parameter choice supplies both analytic growth bounds and coprime arithmetic indices.
- **step-5** [claim](#stmt-prop-ambient-orders) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ambient-orders): Analytic elimination in smooth ambient charts exhibits distinct ordinary orders; isolation also gives reducedness. -> Analytic elimination in smooth ambient charts exhibits distinct ordinary orders; isolation also gives reducedness.
- **step-6** [claim](#stmt-thm-tube) / [proof](#sec:filling-summands): The actual local Milnor lattice is determined by a boundary residue condition, with separately signed inner and exterior forms. -> The actual local Milnor lattice is determined by a boundary residue condition, with separately signed inner and exterior forms.
- **step-7** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary): Proper stratified boundary regularity permits horizontal boundary fibrations on the chosen tube. -> Proper stratified boundary regularity permits horizontal boundary fibrations on the chosen tube.
- **step-8** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuations): The inner and exterior models continue compatibly while preserving labeled interface circles and horizontal complex values. -> The inner and exterior models continue compatibly while preserving labeled interface circles and horizontal complex values.
- **step-9** [claim](#stmt-lem-isolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-isolation): The gradient estimate and strict exponent bounds exclude nonorigin critical points in the tube. -> The gradient estimate and strict exponent bounds exclude nonorigin critical points in the tube.
- **step-10** [claim](#stmt-lem-acyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-acyclic): Circle collapse identifies inner homology and the mod-covering-degree injection; exterior attachment preserves acyclicity. -> Circle collapse identifies inner homology and the mod-covering-degree injection; exterior attachment preserves acyclicity.
- **step-11** [claim](#stmt-lem-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local): Relative homology and boundary regularity identify the large representative with ordinary local Milnor data. -> Relative homology and boundary regularity identify the large representative with ordinary local Milnor data.
- **step-12** [claim](#stmt-lem-mv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mv): The signed exterior cut determines which tensor-product classes extend across the inner piece. -> The signed exterior cut determines which tensor-product classes extend across the inner piece.
- **step-13** [claim](#stmt-lem-bock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bock): The oriented coefficient Bockstein agrees with the full finite-map transfer. -> The oriented coefficient Bockstein agrees with the full finite-map transfer.
- **step-14** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lattice): Subtracting an interface cap determines the fractional inner coordinate and the exact integral residue lattice. -> Subtracting an interface cap determines the fractional inner coordinate and the exact integral residue lattice.
- **step-15** [claim](#stmt-lem-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exterior): Separated fillings yield orthogonality; lifting to the cyclic cover computes the exterior product form. -> Separated fillings yield orthogonality; lifting to the cyclic cover computes the exterior product form.
- **step-16** [claim](#stmt-lem-inner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inner): The normal-disk intersection reverses the plane filling sign on the pushforward kernel. -> The normal-disk intersection reverses the plane filling sign on the pushforward kernel.
- **step-17** [claim](#stmt-lem-parameter-indices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameter-indices): The fixed parameters admit positive congruent indices for the required primitive characters. -> The fixed parameters admit positive congruent indices for the required primitive characters.
- **step-18** [claim](#stmt-lem-arith-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arith-boundary): The axis boundary classes are monodromy fixed and their rational pairings retain the lens-space residue. -> The axis boundary classes are monodromy fixed and their rational pairings retain the lens-space residue.
- **step-19** [claim](#stmt-lem-arith-local-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arith-local-splitting): Normalized boundary classes give integral orthogonal splittings at primes where the corresponding index is a unit. -> Normalized boundary classes give integral orthogonal splittings at primes where the corresponding index is a unit.
- **step-20** [claim](#stmt-lem-arith-discrepancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arith-discrepancy): The discrepancy is confined to primary sectors and has the spin and special-unitary lifts needed for correction. -> The discrepancy is confined to primary sectors and has the spin and special-unitary lifts needed for correction.
- **step-21** [claim](#stmt-lem-arith-real-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arith-real-signs): Holomorphic and antiholomorphic classes give real indefiniteness of the correction forms. -> Holomorphic and antiholomorphic classes give real indefiniteness of the correction forms.
- **step-22** [claim](#stmt-prop-arith-integral-congruence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arith-integral-congruence): Strong approximation patches complementary local isometries while preserving the full integral lattice. -> Strong approximation patches complementary local isometries while preserving the full integral lattice.

If $M_h$ is the monodromy on $H_{\\mathbb Q}=H\\otimes\\mathbb Q$, then $$\\dim_{\\mathbb Q}\\ker(M_h-I)=2,\\qquad\n \\dim_{\\mathbb Q}\\ker\\Phi_5(M_h)=4.$$ The monodromy on either $D_i\\otimes\\mathbb C$ is semisimple at eigenvalue $1$ and at every primitive fifth root of unity.",

Moreover $$\\alpha+\\beta=4,\\qquad a\\alpha=b\\beta,\\qquad\n \\frac{(a-2)\\alpha}{4}>4,\\qquad\n \\frac{(b-2)\\beta}{4}>4.$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **stmt-lem-parameter-indices**: The common parameters admit positive indices with the prescribed congruence and weighted fraction.
- **stmt-lem-cover-adjunction**: Pushforward after full-preimage transfer multiplies the base homology class by the covering degree; adjunction determines a rational orthogonal projection.
- **stmt-prop-arith-integral-congruence**: The actual integral Milnor Seifert lattices of the constructed germs are isometric.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **abstract-form**: Replace the finite-map data by an abstract plane Seifert-form isometry. -> The map-specific boundary residue condition is lost.
- **family**: Interpret ambient homeomorphism as a connecting constant-Milnor-number family. -> The family conclusion would contradict the multiplicity distinction.
- **finite-certificate**: Use a parameter witness computation as a certificate of ambient homeomorphism. -> Arithmetic witnesses leave the analytic, lattice and link steps unchecked.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The positive-index lemma provides a finite constructive arithmetic subresult for each supplied common parameter tuple. Its computation covers that subresult only; the analytic and topological conclusions retain their separate proof obligations.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer r, n0, q.
# PRECONDITIONS: r in [4,6], n0 in [1,512], n0>5*2**r, n0=1 mod 2**r, n0=2 mod 5, q in {1,4}.
# OUTPUTS: JSON a,b,g,m,i,j; j is the least positive admissible j. Invalid input emits REJECT.
# TIME: logarithmic modular inversion in the admitted integer sizes.
# SPACE: constant many exact integers, logarithmic bits in n0.
# ARITHMETIC: exact fmpz integers and fmpq rational inequalities; no floating point.
# FAILURE: REJECT for invalid input; assertion failure for a violated arithmetic invariant.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz, fmpq

def construct(data):
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'r', 'n0', 'q'}:
        return 'REJECT'
    if any(type(data[k]) is not int for k in data):
        return 'REJECT'
    r, n, q = data['r'], data['n0'], data['q']
    if not (4 <= r <= 6 and 1 <= n <= 512 and q in (1, 4)):
        return 'REJECT'
    if not (n > 5 * 2**r and n % 2**r == 1 and n % 5 == 2):
        return 'REJECT'
    N = fmpz(n)
    N1 = N + 5 * 2**r
    A = (N*N - 1) // 4
    B = (N1*N1 - 1) // 4
    G = A.gcd(B)
    M = 4*A*B + A + B
    assert N.gcd(N1) == 1 and N % 4 == N1 % 4 == 1
    assert A % 5 == B % 5 == 2 and G % 5 != 0 and G >= 2**(r-1) > 5
    assert fmpq((A-2)*B, A+B) > 4 and fmpq((B-2)*A, A+B) > 4
    ap, bp = A // G, B // G
    assert M % (5*G) == 0
    target = q*M // (5*G)
    divisor = (ap+bp).gcd(4*bp)
    period = int(4*bp // divisor)
    j = (int(target // divisor) * pow(int((ap+bp)//divisor), -1, period)) % period
    if j == 0:
        j = period
    i = int((target - ap*j) // bp)
    assert i > 0 and j > 0 and (i-j) % 4 == 0 and 5*(B*i+A*j) == q*M
    return dict(a=int(A), b=int(B), g=int(G), m=int(M), i=i, j=j)

try:
    answer = construct(json.loads(sys.stdin.read()))
except (ValueError, TypeError):
    answer = 'REJECT'
print(json.dumps(answer, sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json as J, math as M, sys as S\nfrom pathlib import Path as P\n\ndef exhaustive(x):\n    if type(x) != dict or sorted(x) != ['n0', 'q', 'r']:\n        return 'REJECT'\n    if not all(type(v) == int for v in x.values()):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    exponent, first, branch = x['r'], x['n0'], x['q']\n    if exponent not in range(4, 7) or first not in range(1, 513) or branch not in {1, 4}:\n        return 'REJECT'\n    modulus = 1 << exponent\n    if first <= 5*modulus or (first-1) % modulus or (first-2) % 5:\n        return 'REJECT'\n    second = first + 5*modulus\n    left, right = (first**2-1)//4, (second**2-1)//4\n    common = M.gcd(left, right)\n    weight = 4*left*right + left + right\n    assert M.gcd(first, second) == 1\n    assert min(left, right) > 0 and left % 5 == right % 5 == 2\n    assert common % 5 and common >= (1 << (exponent-1)) and common > 5\n    assert (left-2)*right > 4*(left+right) and (right-2)*left > 4*(left+right)\n    # Exhaust every smaller positive j. The witness i is rechecked by exact multiplication.\n    for jj in range(1, 4*(right//common)+1):\n        numerator = branch*weight - 5*left*jj\n        denominator = 5*right\n        if numerator > 0 and numerator % denominator == 0:\n            ii = numerator // denominator\n            if (ii-jj) % 4 == 0:\n                assert 5*(right*ii+left*jj) == branch*weight\n                return {'a':left, 'b':right, 'g':common, 'm':weight, 'i':ii, 'j':jj}\n    raise AssertionError('no witness in the source bound')\n\ntry:\n    try:\n        payload = J.loads(P(S.argv[1]).read_text())\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        payload = None\n    truth = exhaustive(payload)\n    observed = J.loads(P(S.argv[2]).read_text())\n    expected = J.loads(S.argv[3])\n    assert truth == expected == observed\nexcept Exception:\n    S.exit(1)\nS.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "Return the exact common exponents, greatest common divisor, weight and positive indices satisfying the source congruence and weighted equation, or REJECT for inadmissible input.",
    "reason": "The positive-index lemma provides a finite constructive arithmetic subresult for each supplied common parameter tuple. Its computation covers that subresult only; the analytic and topological conclusions retain their separate proof obligations.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with integer r, n0, q.\n# PRECONDITIONS: r in [4,6], n0 in [1,512], n0>5*2**r, n0=1 mod 2**r, n0=2 mod 5, q in {1,4}.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON a,b,g,m,i,j; j is the least positive admissible j. Invalid input emits REJECT.\n# TIME: logarithmic modular inversion in the admitted integer sizes.\n# SPACE: constant many exact integers, logarithmic bits in n0.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact fmpz integers and fmpq rational inequalities; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: REJECT for invalid input; assertion failure for a violated arithmetic invariant.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz, fmpq\n\ndef construct(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'r', 'n0', 'q'}:\n        return 'REJECT'\n    if any(type(data[k]) is not int for k in data):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    r, n, q = data['r'], data['n0'], data['q']\n    if not (4 <= r <= 6 and 1 <= n <= 512 and q in (1, 4)):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    if not (n > 5 * 2**r and n % 2**r == 1 and n % 5 == 2):\n        return 'REJECT'\n    N = fmpz(n)\n    N1 = N + 5 * 2**r\n    A = (N*N - 1) // 4\n    B = (N1*N1 - 1) // 4\n    G = A.gcd(B)\n    M = 4*A*B + A + B\n    assert N.gcd(N1) == 1 and N % 4 == N1 % 4 == 1\n    assert A % 5 == B % 5 == 2 and G % 5 != 0 and G >= 2**(r-1) > 5\n    assert fmpq((A-2)*B, A+B) > 4 and fmpq((B-2)*A, A+B) > 4\n    ap, bp = A // G, B // G\n    assert M % (5*G) == 0\n    target = q*M // (5*G)\n    divisor = (ap+bp).gcd(4*bp)\n    period = int(4*bp // divisor)\n    j = (int(target // divisor) * pow(int((ap+bp)//divisor), -1, period)) % period\n    if j == 0:\n        j = period\n    i = int((target - ap*j) // bp)\n    assert i > 0 and j > 0 and (i-j) % 4 == 0 and 5*(B*i+A*j) == q*M\n    return dict(a=int(A), b=int(B), g=int(G), m=int(M), i=i, j=j)\n\ntry:\n    answer = construct(json.loads(sys.stdin.read()))\nexcept (ValueError, TypeError):\n    answer = 'REJECT'\nprint(json.dumps(answer, sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conclusion concerns ambient homeomorphism of analytic germs. A software interface, execution semantics and a certified implementation contract are absent. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction compares exact analytic germ topology; it supplies neither a simulation model nor error bounds for discretised scenes. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The existence of an integral Seifert congruence supplies a mathematical conclusion without a specified tool interface or an effective routine for returning the congruence. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Exact arithmetic can check the parameter congruences, coprimality and strict inequalities on a supplied tuple. This inferred use checks arithmetic prerequisites; it does not compute an ambient homeomorphism or the global integral Seifert isometry. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The integral lattice retains a projection-dependent residue condition. It is a mathematical object, without a storage representation or operation costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The analytic and arithmetic proof has no accelerator execution model, memory contract or parallel kernel. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The integral Seifert congruence is a topology result; no adversarial model or computational hardness assumption is provided. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction supplies a topological counterexample rather than a statistical model, training procedure or generalisation guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rational correction is obtained by density within lattice stabilisers. An optimisation objective and a finite optimisation procedure are absent. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Complementary prime-local lattice isometries are combined by arithmetic approximation. No process model or communication protocol is supplied. |
| other (Singularity theory and embedded topology) | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The paper directly concerns singularity theory and embedded topology: ambient pair germs can be homeomorphic while their multiplicities differ. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **ambient-scope** (counterexample_scope): The ambient three-variable case remains untreated. Blocked use: ['Extending this counterexample to the ambient three-variable case.']
- **pair-not-family** (counterexample_scope): Ambient equivalence of this pair does not supply a coefficientwise continuous path of formal power series with finite constant Milnor number. Blocked use: ['Using the constructed pair as the endpoints of a constant-Milnor-number deformation.']
- **contact-scope** (counterexample_scope): The comparison uses smooth open books and integral Seifert forms; preservation of the natural contact structures is not established. Blocked use: ['Treating the ambient equivalence as an embedded contactomorphism.']
- **projection-data** (hypothesis): The abstract plane-form isometry does not assume compatibility with the projections to the common base. The projection-dependent boundary condition must be retained. Blocked use: ['Replacing the finite-map gluing data by an abstract Seifert-form isometry alone.']
- **qualifying-parameters** (hypothesis): The tube calculation requires sufficiently large qualifying exponents and a sufficiently small positive deformation parameter. Blocked use: ['Applying isolation or the tube lattice formula at arbitrary exponents or deformation parameters.']
- **boundary-value-preservation** (edge_case): The inner continuation preserves the complex value on the horizontal boundary; this requirement does not extend through the critical region. Blocked use: ['Assuming a value-preserving trivialisation through the inner critical region.']
- **auxiliary-interface** (edge_case): Outer boundary regularity does not assert disk-wide submersivity on the auxiliary interface. Blocked use: ['Applying outer-stratum submersivity directly to the auxiliary interface over the entire value disk.']
- **global-isometry-existence** (nonconstructive): The global correction is obtained from density in an adelic neighbourhood. An effective routine and a computational resource bound for returning that correction are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Claiming a finite implemented construction of the global integral isometry from the density argument alone.']
- **link-classification** (hypothesis): The link classification requires simple spinnable structures on the specified sphere and congruent integral Seifert matrices. Blocked use: ['Applying the classification to arbitrary links or to rational Seifert isometry alone.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a negative answer to Zariski's multiplicity question in four complex variables. We construct two reduced convergent holomorphic function germs with isolated critical points and multiplicities four and five whose zero-set germs are ambiently homeomorphic.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-lem-simultaneous-parameters](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-parameters) | lemma | `lem:simultaneous-parameters` | 4 | For every sufficiently large integer $r$, choose an integer $n_0$ such that |
